This caused all sensor data to have identical timestamps.\n\nProblem flow:\n1. OralableDevice creates timestamps with 20ms offsets (50Hz sampling)\n   - Sample 0: 11:13:02.000\n   - Sample 1: 11:13:02.020\n   - Sample 2: 11:13:02.040\n\n2. SensorDataProcessor rounded to 100ms buckets:\n   round(timestamp * 10) / 10\n   - Sample 0: 11:13:02.000 → 11:13:02.0\n   - Sample 1: 11:13:02.020 → 11:13:02.0 (SAME!)\n   - Sample 2: 11:13:02.040 → 11:13:02.0 (SAME!)\n\n3. All samples grouped by rounded timestamp → single SensorData entry\n4. Aggregator sees all data with same timestamp → 1 data point\n\nSolution:\n- Removed timestamp rounding in 3 locations:\n  1. updatePPGHistory() - line 159\n  2. updateAccelHistory() - line 214\n  3. updateLegacySensorData() - line 249\n- Now preserves exact microsecond-precision timestamps\n- Each 20ms sample maintains unique timestamp\n\nAdded comprehensive logging:\n- SensorDataProcessor shows exact timestamps when adding data\n- clearHistory() confirms removal count\n- HistoricalDataAggregator shows data span and timestamp range\n\nFiles changed:\n- SensorDataProcessor.swift: Remove all timestamp rounding\n- HistoricalDataModels.swift: Add timestamp verification logging\n\nThis fix resolves the 2-day issue where historical data showed only\n1 aggregated point despite hundreds of sensor readings.\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude <noreply@anthropic.com>"[m[1;37m,[m

      [1;34m"message"[m[1;37m:[m [32m"Fix fan-in bias in boolean popcount scoring: 17.40% -> 89.13% on MNIST test set\n\nThe boolean output layer scored each class by raw popcount of satisfied\nAND-literals, but class fan-ins range from 5 (digit 1) to 18 (digit 8),\nso argmax was biased toward high-fan-in classes: the classifier predicted\ndigit 8 almost constantly (974/974 on 8s, 0/1135 on 1s).\n\nAdd per-class offsets (max_fanin - fanin) so that\nscore = max_fanin - violated_literals: fewest violations wins on a shared\nscale, mirroring the per-neuron offsets the weighted path already used.\nApplied consistently in the Verilog emitter, evaluate.py's score-based\nevaluation, and the (new) boolean-path verifier.\n\nverify_verilog_match.py now also covers the boolean export with a hard\npass/fail assertion (fail if more than 5 points below the STE reference)\nso this class of regression cannot ship silently again; --checkpoint is\nnow required instead of defaulting to a stale model.\n\nVerified three ways, all agreeing at exactly 8913/10000 (89.13%):\n- Python simulation of the export semantics (verify_verilog_match.py --boolean)\n- evaluate.py score-based rule evaluation\n- Icarus Verilog simulation of the regenerated netlist over all 10k images\n\nFull ladder for lnn_OR:512,AND:10_best.pt: continuous 86.02%, STE 85.42%,\n8-bit threshold 85.34%, fixed boolean popcount 89.13%, strict AND/OR 52.61%.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>"[m[1;37m,[m

Added\nparseIstTimestamp(), which parses the components explicitly against\nthe known IST offset instead of relying on ambiguous local-timezone\nstring parsing, and wired it into both the webhook's callDate and\nthe worker's Call Details startTime lookup."[m[1;37m,[m
